$88 + \boxed{\phantom{23}} = 173$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
88 + a              &= 173 \\\\
a                   &= 173 - 88 \\\\
a                   &= 85
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 85$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$85$

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$\boxed{\phantom{23}} \times 0.25 = 1$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 0.25                   &= 1 \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {1} {0.25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {100} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {25 \times 4} {25} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {25} \times 4} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 4$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>

a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$64 \div 8 = 32 \div \boxed{\phantom{23}}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the missing number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
64 \div 8                       &= 32 \div a \\\\
\dfrac {64} {8}                 &= \dfrac {32} {a} \\\\
a                               &= 32 \times \dfrac {8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 8} {64} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {32 \times 2 \times 4} {32 \times 2} \\\\
a                               &= \dfrac {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2 \times 4} {\cancel {32} \times \cancel 2} \\\\
a                               &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

The missing number $= 4$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>

\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A054337A-52DB-4666-A80E-C41FDDC92EB8'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

My four pet monkeys collected a pile of $60$ peanuts.

Monkey $A$ woke in the night and ate half of them,
then Monkey $B$ woke and ate one third of what was left, 
then Monkey $C$ woke and ate one quarter of the rest 
and finally Monkey $D$ woke and ate one fifth of what remainder.

</div>

<div class='working'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many peanuts did Monkey $D$ eat?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey A}          &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {60} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {15} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Monkey D ate $= 3$ peanuts.

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \left(\dfrac {3} {4} \times \left(\dfrac {2} {3} \times \left(\dfrac {1} {2} \times 60 \right) \right) \right) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times \dfrac {3} {4} \times \dfrac {2} {3} \times \dfrac {1} {2} \times 60  \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {1 \times 3 \times 2 \times 1 \times 60} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 2 \times 5 \times 4 \times 3} {5 \times 4 \times 3 \times 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times \cancel 4 \times \cancel 3 \times \cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

Monkey D ate $= 3$ peanuts.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey B}          &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times (60 - 30) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {30} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 3} {3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel 3} {\cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 10 \\\\
\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey C}          &= \dfrac {1} {4} \times (60 - 30 - 10) \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 4} {4} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\

\text{Peanuts ate by Monkey D}          &= \dfrac {1} {5} \times (60 - 30 - 10 - 5) \\\\

$

One quarter chose tea.

Seven people chose coffee.

$30 \%$ of them chose cola.

The rest chose water.

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many people chose water?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of people who choose water}    &= 20 - \dfrac {1} {4} \times 20 - 7 - 30 \% \ \text{of} \ 20 \\\\
                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                            &= 20 - \dfrac {20} {4} - 7 - \dfrac {30} {100} \times 20 \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} - \dfrac {30 \times 20} {100} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} - \dfrac {3 \cancel 0 \times 2 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                            &= 13 - 5 - 3 \times 2 \\\\
                                            &= 8 - 6 \\\\
                                            &= 2
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Maya drew a pie chart to show all their choices. How many degrees should **"Water"** have on her pie chart?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Degrees for water on chart}           &= \dfrac {2} {20} \times 360 \ \text{degrees} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 360} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times 20} {20} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {2 \times 18 \times \cancel {20}} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                            &= 2 \times 18 \\\\
                                            &= 36 \ \text{degrees}
\end{aligned}
$

Water should have $36 \ \text{degrees}$ on the pie chart.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36 \ \text{degrees}$

</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The difference between $\dfrac{1}{3}$ of a certain number and $\dfrac{1}{4}$ of the same number is 3. 

What is the number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$

The number $= 36$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$36$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {3} \times a - \dfrac {1} {4} \times a                       &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {3} - \dfrac {a} {4}                                         &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {a \times 4} {3 \times 4} - \dfrac {a \times 3} {4 \times 3}     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a} {12} - \dfrac {3a} {12}                                     &= 3 \\\\
\dfrac {4a - 3a} {12}                                                   &= 3 \\\\
4a - 3a                                                                 &= 3 \times 12 \\\\
a                                                                       &= 36
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number $= 36$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$36$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

$383$ and $6226$ are examples of palindromic numbers as they read the 
same when the order of their digits is reversed. 

Write down the largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Unit place digit of multiple of $5$ $= 0, 5$

As per the question,

Number is palindromic so,

Digit at unit place $= $ Digit at $10000$ th place.

The largest five-digit palindromic number that is a multiple of $5 = 59995$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$59995$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Unit place digit of multiple of $5$ $= 0, 5$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
Digit at unit place $= $ Digit at $10000$ th place.
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The order of the digits is reversed in a certain two-digit whole number. 

This gives a new whole number which is one less than half of the original number. What is the original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the digit at ones place $= a$

Digit at tens place $= b$

Original number $= 10b + a$

Reversed number $= 10a + b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out a number that satisfies the above equation.

|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |

The digit at ones place $= 2$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

The Original number $= 52$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$52$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the digit at ones place $= a$
<emptyline>
Digit at tens place $= b$
<emptyline>
Original number $= 10b + a$
<emptyline>
Reversed number $= 10a + b$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {1} {2} \times (10b + a) - 1 \\\\
10a + b                     &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} - 1 \\\\
10a + b + 1                 &= \dfrac {10b + a} {2} \\\\
2 \times (10a + b + 1)      &= 10b + a \\\\
20a + 2b + 2                &= 10b + a \\\\
2                           &= 10b - 2b + a - 20a \\\\
2                           &= 8b - 19a \\\\
8b - 19a                    &= 2 \\\\
8b                          &= 19a + 2 \\\\
b                           &= \dfrac {19a + 2} {8}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Lets find out a number that satisfies the above equation.
<emptyline>
|  a  |          19a + 2        | Multiple of 8 |
|:---:|:-----------------------:|:-------------:|
| $1$ |  $19 \times 1 + 2 = 21$ |      No       |
| $2$ |  $19 \times 2 + 2 = 40$ |      Yes      |
<emptyline>
The digit at ones place $= 2$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{The digit at tens place}          &= \dfrac {19 \times 2 + 2} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {40} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times 8} {8} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {5 \times \cancel 8} {\cancel 8} \\\\
                                        &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The Original number $= 52$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DC8E8DED-85CC-4567-AA5D-5302654F696D'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Every digit of a certain positive whole number is either a $3$ or $4$, with each occurring at least one. 

The number divides exactly by both $3$ and $4$. 

What is the smallest number it could be? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Using trial and error,

Smallest required number $= 3444$

Let's verify.

$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3444} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 287} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 287} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
&= 287
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$3444$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Using trial and error,
<emptyline>
Smallest required number $= 3444$
<emptyline>
Let's verify.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
&= \dfrac {3444} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {3 \times 4 \times 287} {3 \times 4} \\\\
&= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4 \times 287} {\cancel 3 \times \cancel 4} \\\\
&= 287
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$3444$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-58BAFA2D-B38E-4A53-8822-FF8913C2BE37'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Hareni has twice as many sweets as Dora. 
After eating $18$ sweets each, she has five times as many as Dora. How many sweets did Hareni start with?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$

Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$48$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let at the start number of sweets Dora has $= a$
<emptyline>
Number of sweets Hareni has $= 2a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
5 \times (a - 18)       &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 5 \times 18        &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 90                 &= 2a - 18 \\\\
5a - 2a                 &= 90 - 18 \\\\
3a                      &= 72 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {72} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 24} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 24} {\cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of sweets Hareni has}       &= 24 \times 2 \\\\
                                         &= 48
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$48$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>




i) Remove \"then\" from the second line

 

ii) Start the working in an opposite way like below, so that there will be no need of swapping the terms. 

5 \times (a - 18)         &= 2a - 18 \\

5 \times a - 5 \times 18  &= 2a - 18 \\

...
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Three cereal bars, two apples and one tube of mints cost $\pounds 3.14$.

Two cereal bars, three apples and four tube of mints cost $\pounds 4.21$.

What is the total cost of buying one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mints?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 

Price of one apple $= a$

Price of one tube of mint $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$

Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.47$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the price of one cereal bar $= c$ 
<emptyline>
Price of one apple $= a$
<emptyline>
Price of one tube of mint $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m             &= 3.14 \\\\
2c + 3a + 4m            &= 4.21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's add both the equations.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
3c + 2a + m + 2c + 3a + 4m          &= 3.14 + 4.21 \\\\
5c + 5a + 5m                        &= 7.35 \\\\
5 (c + a + m)                       &= \dfrac {735} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {5 \times 147} {100 \times 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 147} {100 \times \cancel 5} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \dfrac {147} {100} \\\\
c + a + m                           &= \pounds 1.47
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total cost of one cereal bar, one apple and one tube of mint $= \pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.47$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Each letter stands for a different number.

The totals for each row and column are shown.

![missing image](/papers/missing_image.svg)


What is the value of each letter?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of A $= a$

Value of B $= b$

Value of C $= c$

Value of D $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
d + d + d + d           &= 26 \\\\
4d                      &= 26 \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26} {4} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {26 \times 25} {4 \times 25} \\\\
d                       &= \dfrac {650} {100} \\\\
d                       &= 6.5 \\\\
c + c + c + d           &= 17 \\\\
3c + d                  &= 17 \\\\
3c + 6.5                &= 17 \\\\
3c                      &= 17 - 6.5 \\\\
c                       &= \dfrac {10.5} {3} \\\\
c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\
a + 12.5                &= 22 \\\\
a                       &= 22 - 12.5 \\\\
a                       &= 9.5
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Value of A}           &= 9.5 \\\\
\text{Value of B}           &= 2.5 \\\\
\text{Value of C}           &= 3.5 \\\\
\text{Value of D}           &= 6.5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text{Value of A} = 9.5$

$\text{Value of B} = 2.5$

$\text{Value of C} = 3.5$

$\text{Value of D} = 6.5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of A $= a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>

c                       &= 3.5 \\\\
b + b + b + c           &= 11 \\\\
3b + c                  &= 11 \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - c \\\\
3b                      &= 11 - 3.5 \\\\
b                       &= \dfrac {7.5} {3} \\\\
b                       &= 2.5 \\\\
a + b + c + d           &= 22 \\\\
a + 2.5 + 3.5 + 6.5     &= 22 \\\\

\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$

$
</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of five consecutive whole numbers is $100$. 

What is the smallest of these five numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the five consecutive whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a - 2 + a - 1 + a + a + 1 + a + 2   &= 100 \\\\
a + a + a + a + a - 2 + 2 - 1 + 1   &= 100 \\\\
5a                                  &= 100 \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {100} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest whole number}        &= a - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$18$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the five consecutive whole numbers $= a - 2, a - 1, a , a + 1, a + 2$
<emptyline>

a                                   &= \dfrac {100} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times 5} {5} \\\\
a                                   &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel 5} {\cancel 5} \\\\
a                                   &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Smallest whole number}        &= a - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 20 - 2 \\\\
                                    &= 18
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$18$
</code></pre>
</div>

</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The sum of two numbers is $22$ and their difference is $4$. 

What are the two numbers?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the two numbers $= a, b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b       &= 22 \\\\
a - b       &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Let's add both the equations.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b + a - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
a + a + b - b           &= 22 + 4 \\\\
2a                      &= 26 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

Let's substitute the value of a in one of the initial equations to find b.

$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

The two numbers $= 13, 9$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$13, 9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the two numbers $= a, b$
<emptyline>

\begin{aligned}

\end{aligned}

a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 13} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Let's substitute the value of a in one of the initial equations to find b.
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a + b                   &= 22 \\\\
b                       &= 22 - a \\\\
b                       &= 22 - 13 \\\\
b                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The two numbers $= 13, 9$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$13, 9$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-EB2674B4-189E-4F68-A98E-E3E6353FB1E6'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

At Ali's shop, a multipack of $8$ toilet rolls costs $\pounds 2.40$. 

At Mya's shop, the cost per toilet roll is twice as much. 

How much is a pack of $3$ toilet rolls at Mya's shop?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}       &= 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 1.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Ali's shop}       &= \dfrac {2.4} {8} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {24} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {8 \times 3} {8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel 8 \times 3} {\cancel 8 \times 10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.3
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost per toilet roll at Mya's shop}       &= 0.3 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 0.6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Cost of 3 toilet rolls at Mya's shop}       &= 0.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                                  &= \pounds 1.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 1.8$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8FD4F287-E8F1-42F2-B909-FC3B822573A4'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Paddy is leading in a race. 

He is $81 \ \text{metres}$ in front of Sam who is in last place. 

Martin is between Paddy and Sam. 

Martin is $40 \ \text{metres}$ from the finish line and is twice as far away from Sam as he is from Paddy. 

How far has Sam left to run?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$

Distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a + 81 - 40             &= 2 \times (40 - a) \\\\
a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= 13 + 81 \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$94 \ \text{metres}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let distance of Paddy from finish line $= a$
<emptyline>
Distance of Sam from finish line $= a + 81$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}

a + 41                  &= 80 - 2a \\\\
a + 2a                  &= 80 - 41 \\\\
3a                      &= 39 \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {39} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 13} {3} \\\\
a                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 13} { \cancel 3} \\\\
a                       &= 13
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Distance of Sam from the finish line}     &= 13 + 81 \\\\
                                                &= 94 \ \text{metres}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$94 \ \text{metres}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

Remove \"then\" from the second line, just write Distance of Sam from finish line = a + 81=a+81



Remove \"metres\" from below line:

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I thought of a number, halved it and then subtracted $9$.

I got an answer of $12$.

What was my original number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the original number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$

The original number $= 42$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the original number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The original number $= 42$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Fawzan went shopping with $\pounds 100$ in his wallet.

He spent $10 \%$ of his money on a book and $20 \%$ of the rest on a DVD. How much money did he then have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on book}              &= 10 \% \ \text{of} \ 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left after buying books}            &= 100 - 10 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on DVD}               &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}        &= 90 - 18 \\\\
                                                    &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}            &= 80 \% \ \text{of} \ \left( 90 \% \ \text{of} \ \pounds 100 \right) \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times \dfrac {90} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {80 \times 90 \times 100} {100 \times 100} \\\\
                                                        &= \dfrac {8 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                        &= 8 \times 9 \\\\
                                                        &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}

                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}

                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Abdul is making some small snacks for a party.

He cuts $1.2 \ \text{kg}$ of cheese into a number of $30 \ \text{g}$ pieces.

How many pieces does he have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}         &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-C507590F-A02C-4460-82D7-E935ED9CB61C'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

%waffles%

Given that $\spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

and $\spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

and $\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

how many $\spadesuit$ are equal to $\spadesuit$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of triangle $= t$

Value of square $= s$

Value of circle $= c$

Value of diamond $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$

$6$ triangles are equal to a diamond.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of triangle $= t$
<emptyline>
Value of square $= s$
<emptyline>
Value of circle $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of diamond $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$6$ triangles are equal to a diamond.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='review-comments'>

<h4>Review Comments</h4>




Remove \"then the\" from all the definitions. Write like this

\"Value of square = s\"

\"Value of circle = c\"

.....
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the value of $9 * 8$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 9 * 8 \\\\
&= (5 \times 9) + 8 \\\\
&= 45 + 8 \\\\
&= 53
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='answers'>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What number does $m$ stand for, if $m * 6 = 41$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
m * 6                   &= 41 \\\\
(5 \times m) + 6        &= 41 \\\\
5m                      &= 41 - 6 \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {35} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {5 \times 7} {5} \\\\
m                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 7} {\cancel 5} \\\\
m                       &= 7
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$7$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $n * 4 = 8 * n$, what number does $n$ stand for?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
n * 4                   &= 8 * n \\\\
(5 \times n) + 4        &= (5 \times 8) + n \\\\
5n + 4                  &= 40 + n \\\\
5n - n                  &= 40 - 4 \\\\
4n                      &= 36 \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 9} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4} \\\\
n                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$9$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

4n                      &= 36 \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {36} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {4 \times 9} {4} \\\\
n                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 9} {\cancel 4} \\\\
n                       &= 9
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

I thought of a number, halved it and then subtracted $9$.

I got an answer of $12$.

What was my original number ?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the original number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$

The original number $= 42$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$42$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the original number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {a} {2} - 9              &= 12 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 12 + 9 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2}                  &= 21 \\\\
a                               &= 2 \times 21 \\\\
a                               &= 42 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The original number $= 42$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$42$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Fawzan went shopping with $\pounds 100$ in his wallet.

He spent $10 \%$ of his money on a book and $20 \%$ of the rest on a DVD. How much money did he then have left?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on book}              &= 10 \% \ \text{of} \ 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times \cancel {100}} {\cancel {100}} \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 10
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left after buying books}            &= 100 - 10 \\\\
                                                &= \pounds 90
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money spent on DVD}               &= 20 \% \ \text{of} \ 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20} {100} \times 90 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {20 \times 90} {100} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {2 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                        &= 2 \times 9 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 18
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shooping}            &= 90 - 18 \\\\
                                                        &= \pounds 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Alternate solution.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Money left with Fawzan after shopping}        &= 80 \% \ \text{of} \ \left( 90 \% \ \text{of} \ \pounds 100 \right) \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {80} {100} \times \dfrac {90} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {80 \times 90 \times 100} {100 \times 100} \\\\
                                                    &= \dfrac {8 \cancel 0 \times 9 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0 \times \cancel {100}} \\\\
                                                    &= 8 \times 9 \\\\
                                                    &= 72
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 72$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

                                        &= \dfrac {10} {100} \times 100 \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {10 \times 100} {100} \\\\

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Abdul is making some small snacks for a party.

He cuts $1.2 \ \text{kg}$ of cheese into a number of $30 \ \text{g}$ pieces.

How many pieces does he have?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}         &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total weight of cheese}       &= 1.2 \ \text{kg} \\\\
                                    &= 1.2 \times 1000 \ \text{g} \\\\
                                    &= 1200 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of 30 g pieces}         &= \dfrac {1200} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {30 \times 40} {30} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {30} \times 40} {\cancel {30}} \\\\
                                &= 40
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-25C8E6E5-75DD-4C5C-B894-4B9EB3D5C56F'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

%waffles%

Given that $\spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

and $\spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

and $\spadesuit + \spadesuit + \spadesuit = \spadesuit$

how many $\spadesuit$s are equal to $\spadesuit$?


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the value of triangle $= t$

Value of square $= s$

Value of circle $= c$

Value of diamond $= d$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$

$6$ triangles are equal to a diamond.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the value of triangle $= t$
<emptyline>
Value of square $= s$
<emptyline>
Value of circle $= c$
<emptyline>
Value of diamond $= d$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2t              &= s \\\\
s + t           &= c \\\\
d               &= c + s + t \\\\
c               &= 2t + t \\\\
c               &= 3t \\\\
d               &= 3t + 2t + t \\\\
d               &= 6t
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$6$ triangles are equal to a diamond.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

Maisie makes $30$ small boxes of mixed sweets to sell at the school summer fair. 
Each box has $1$ Pineapple Jelly, $2$ Lemon Sours and $3$ Raspberry Creams.

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many packets of each type of sweet does she need to buy?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams}       &= 30 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies}      &= 30 \times 1 \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours}            &= 30 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 60
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams packets}       &= \dfrac {90} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {15 \times 6} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 6} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies packets}      &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours packets}            &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Raspberry creams packets $= 6$

Pineapple jellies packets $= 3$

Lemon sours packets $= 3$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams}       &= 30 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= 90 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies}      &= 30 \times 1 \\\\
                                        &= 30 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours}            &= 30 \times 2 \\\\
                                        &= 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of raspberry creams packets}       &= \dfrac {90} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {15 \times 6} {15} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {15} \times 6} {\cancel {15}} \\\\
                                                &= 6 \\\\
\text{Number of pineapple jellies packets}      &= \dfrac {30} {10} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {3 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0} \\\\
                                                &= 3 \\\\
\text{Number of lemon sours packets}            &= \dfrac {60} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3} {20} \\\\
                                                &= \dfrac {\cancel {20} \times 3} {\cancel {20}} \\\\
                                                &= 3
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Raspberry creams packets $= 6$

Pineapple jellies packets $= 3$

Lemon sours packets $= 3$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

\text{Cost of raspberry creams}         &= 1.65 \times 6 \\\\

\text{Cost of pineapple jellies}        &= 1.5 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 4.5 \\\\
\text{Cost of lemon sours}              &= 1.6 \times 3 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 4.8
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total cost}               &= 9.9 + 4.5 + 4.8 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 19.2
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If she sells her boxes for $\pounds 1$ each, how much profit will she make?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Selling Price}            &= 1 \times 30 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 30 \\\\
\text{Cost Price}               &= \pounds 19.2 \\\\
\text{Profit}                   &= \text{Selling Price} - \text{Cost Price} \\\\
                                &= 30 - 19.2 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 10.8
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 10.8$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

                                &= \pounds 30 \\\\
\text{Cost Price}               &= \pounds 19.2 \\\\
\text{Profit}                   &= \text{Selling Price} - \text{Cost Price} \\\\
                                &= 30 - 19.2 \\\\
                                &= \pounds 10.8
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-A0F502EF-3E48-4DA9-AB5F-D68A04931962'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

I roll a bouncy ball down the stairs. It bounces once after $2$ steps, then again after double the number of steps. 
It carries on falling, bouncing after triple the number of steps it had bounced originally. 
At this point how many steps lower is the ball than where it started?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of steps before 1st bounce}        &= 2 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 2nd bounce}        &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 4 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 3rd bounce}        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of steps}        &= 2 + 4 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

Number of steps the ball lowered after it started rolling $= 12$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of steps before 1st bounce}        &= 2 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 2nd bounce}        &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
                                                &= 4 \\\\
\text{Number of steps before 3rd bounce}        &= 2 \times 3 \\\\
                                                &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total number of steps}        &= 2 + 4 + 6 \\\\
                                    &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of steps the ball lowered after it started rolling $= 12$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-8822FB75-BF00-40E2-AB22-0DB492E3E800'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

The symbol $\spadesuit$ placed between two numbers means “multiply together then add three”.

For example: $3 \ \spadesuit \ 5 = 3 \times 5 + 3 = 15 + 3 = 18$

Another new mathematical symbol has also been invented.

$\bigstar$ means “multiply together, unless the answer would be odd in which case add together instead”.

So $3  \ \bigstar \  4 = 12$ (because $3 \times 4$ is even)

While $5   \ \bigstar \   7 = 12$ (because $5 \times 7$ would be odd, so we add $5$ and $7$ instead!) 

</div>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is $5 \ \spadesuit \ 10$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 5 \ \spadesuit \ 10 \\\\
&= 5 \times 10 + 3 \\\\
&= 50 + 3 \\\\
&= 53
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$53$

</div>
</div>

<emptyline>

&= 5 \times 10 + 3 \\\\
&= 50 + 3 \\\\
&= 53
\end{aligned}
$

<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $x  \ \spadesuit \  4 = 23$, what number is $x$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
x  \ \spadesuit \  4        &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4 + 3              &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4                  &= 23 - 3 \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
x                           &= 5
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$5$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>

x \times 4 + 3              &= 23 \\\\
x \times 4                  &= 23 - 3 \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {20} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {4 \times 5} {4} \\\\
x                           &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 5} {\cancel 4} \\\\
x                           &= 5
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
y  \ \spadesuit \  y        &= 39 \\\\
y \times y + 3              &= 39 \\\\
y \times y                  &= 39 - 3 \\\\
y ^ 2                       &= 36 \\\\
y                           &= \sqrt {36} \\\\
y                           &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

If $y   \ \bigstar \   3 = 24$, there are two possible values of $y$. What are they?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

Case $1$:

$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y \times 3              &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
y                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$

The first value of y $= 8$

Case $2$:

$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y + 3                   &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= 24 - 3 \\\\
y                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$

The second value of y $= 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

The two values of y $= 8, 21$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,

y                       &= \dfrac {24} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {3 \times 8} {3} \\\\
y                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times 8} {\cancel 3} \\\\
y                       &= 8
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
y \ \bigstar \ 3        &= 24 \\\\
y + 3                   &= 24 \\\\
y                       &= 24 - 3 \\\\
y                       &= 21
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The second value of y $= 21$
</code></pre>
</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-7254B1AB-7F46-4CE7-83B9-1781BE9D4FCE'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

Olly thinks of a positive whole number. When he divides $60$ by his number, the answer is also a whole number.
How many different numbers could Olly have thought of?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 60}    &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60
\end{aligned}
$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Different numbers Olly thought of}        &= \text{Number of factors of 60} \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$12$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Factors of 60}    &= 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 10, 12, 15, 20, 30, 60
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Different numbers Olly thought of}        &= \text{Number of factors of 60} \\\\
                                                &= 12
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$12$
</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha'>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the mass of Patrick who has a height of $1.5 \ \text{m}$ and a BMI of $20$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of Patrick (in kg) $= m$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
20                                      &= \dfrac {m} {1.5 ^ 2} \\\\
20 \times 1.5 \times 1.5                &= m \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 15} {10 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 3 \times 15} {2 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 5 \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
m                                       &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
m                                       &= 45 \ \text{kg} 
\end{aligned}
$

Mass of Patrick $= 45 \ \text{kg}$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$45 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the mass of Patrick (in kg) $= m$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
20                                      &= \dfrac {m} {1.5 ^ 2} \\\\
20 \times 1.5 \times 1.5                &= m \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {20 \times 15 \times 15} {10 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {2 \times 10 \times 5 \times 3 \times 15} {2 \times 5 \times 10} \\\\
m                                       &= \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times \cancel {10} \times \cancel 5 \times 3 \times 15} {\cancel 2 \times \cancel 5 \times \cancel {10}} \\\\
m                                       &= 3 \times 15 \\\\
m                                       &= 45 \ \text{kg} 
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of Patrick $= 45 \ \text{kg}$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$45 \ \text{kg}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Find the BMI of Bess who is $1.2 \ \text{m}$ tall and has a mass of $36 \ \text{kg}$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Body Mass Index (BMI)}            &= \dfrac {36} {1.2 ^ 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 \times 10 \times 10} {12 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2 \times 5} {12 \times 2 \times 2 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 2 \times 5} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                        &= 25
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$25$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Body Mass Index (BMI)}            &= \dfrac {36} {1.2 ^ 2} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {36 \times 10 \times 10} {12 \times 12} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 12 \times 2 \times 5 \times 2 \times 5} {12 \times 2 \times 2 \times 3} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel 3 \times \cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times 5 \times \cancel 2 \times 5} {\cancel {12} \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 2 \times \cancel 3} \\\\
                                        &= 5 \times 5 \\\\
                                        &= 25
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>

<p class='review_status initial' id='SUBTAG-STATUS-DDAFB51E-6FC3-4231-8E4A-12B8D03865C5'>Initial</p>
<div class='question question'>

**Mobile phone calls**

My monthly mobile phone bill is in two parts: a fixed $\pounds 10$ service charge plus $5$ pence for each call I make.

In October my bill was $\pounds 21.00$.

In November I made twice as many calls as in October.

Work out the cost of my November bill.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Charge of phone calls}        &= \text{Total bill} - \text{Service charge} \\\\
                                    &= 21 - 10 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$

As per question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Phone calls charge in November}        &= 2 \times \text{Phone calls charge in October} \\\\
                                             &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 22
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total of November bill}           &= \text{Service charge} + \text{Phone calls charge} \\\\
                                        &= 10 + 22 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 32$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Charge of phone calls}        &= \text{Total bill} - \text{Service charge} \\\\
                                    &= 21 - 10 \\\\
                                    &= \pounds 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
As per question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Phone calls charge in November}        &= 2 \times \text{Phone calls charge in October} \\\\
                                             &= 2 \times 11 \\\\
                                             &= \pounds 22
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Total of November bill}           &= \text{Service charge} + \text{Phone calls charge} \\\\
                                        &= 10 + 22 \\\\
                                        &= \pounds 32
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\pounds 32$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

**Sweets**

Luke, Fred and Evie have a bag of sweets.

Luke eats half of the sweets.

Fred eats two-thirds of what is left.

Evie eats the remaining $24$ sweets.

Work out how many sweets were in the bag to begin with.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of sweets at the start $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times a \right)        &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times a                       &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3}                                             &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {6}                                                      &= 24 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24 \times 6 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 144
\end{aligned}
$

The number of sweets in the bag at the start $= 144$.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$144$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of sweets at the start $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \left( \dfrac {1} {3} \times a \right)        &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {1} {2} \times \dfrac {1} {3} \times a                       &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {2 \times 3}                                             &= 24 \\\\
\dfrac {a} {6}                                                      &= 24 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 24 \times 6 \\\\
a                                                                   &= 144
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
The number of sweets in the bag at the start $= 144$.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$144$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
